   Synonyms

fleet

   ^1 American
   (BUTTON) [fleet] / flit /

noun

    1. the largest organized unit of naval ships grouped for tactical or
       other purposes.
    2. the largest organization of warships under the command of a single
       officer.
    3. a number of naval vessels or vessels carrying armed crew members.
    4. a large group of ships, airplanes, trucks, etc., operated by a
       single company or under the same ownership.

     He owns a fleet of cabs.
    5. a large group of airplanes, automobiles, etc., moving or operating
       together.
     __________________________________________________________________

   fleet ^2 American
   (BUTTON) [fleet] / flit /

adjective

   fleeter, fleetest
    1. swift; rapid.

     to be fleet of foot;
     a fleet horse.
     __________________________________________________________________

verb (used without object)

    1. to move swiftly; fly.
    2. Nautical. to change position; shift.
    3. Archaic.
         1. to glide along like a stream.
         2. to fade; vanish.
    4. Obsolete. to float; drift; swim.

verb (used with object)

    1. to cause (time) to pass lightly or swiftly.

        Synonyms:
                beguile, hasten, speed

    2. Nautical.
         1. to move or change the position of.
         2. to separate the blocks of (a tackle).
         3. to lay (a rope) along a deck.

   fleet ^3 American
   (BUTTON) [fleet] / flit /

noun

   British Dialect.
    1. an arm of the sea; inlet.
    2. a creek; stream; watercourse.
    3. the Fleet, a former prison in London, long used for debtors.
     __________________________________________________________________

   fleet ^1 British
   / fli:t /

adjective

    1. rapid in movement; swift
    2. poetic fleeting; transient

verb

    1. (intr) to move rapidly
    2. archaic (intr) to fade away smoothly; glide
    3. (tr) nautical
         1. to change the position of (a hawser)
         2. to pass (a messenger or lead) to a hawser from a winch for
            hauling in
         3. to spread apart (the blocks of a tackle)
    4. obsolete (intr) to float or swim
    5. obsolete (tr) to cause (time) to pass rapidly

   fleet ^2 British
   / fli:t /

noun

    1. a number of warships organized as a tactical unit
    2. all the warships of a nation
    3. a number of aircraft, ships, buses, etc, operating together or
       under the same ownership

   "Collins English Dictionary -- Complete &amp; Unabridged" 2012 Digital

   Fleet ^3 British
   / fli:t /

noun

    1. a stream that formerly ran into the Thames between Ludgate Hill and
       Fleet Street and is now a covered sewer
    2. Also called: Fleet Prison.  (formerly) a London prison, esp used
       for holding debtors

Other Word Forms

     * fleetly adverb
     * fleetness noun

Etymology

Origin of fleet^1

   First recorded before 1000; Middle English flete, fleot(e), Old English
   fleot "bay, estuary; boat," derivative of fleotan float ( def. ); fleet
   ^2

Origin of fleet^2

   First recorded in 1520-30; probably from or akin to Old Norse fljtr
   "quick, speedy"

Origin of fleet^3

   First recorded before 900; Middle English flete, Old English fleot
   "flowing water"; cognate with German Fliess "brook"; fleet ^3 def. 3 is
   so called after the Fleet a stream, later covered and used as a sewer,
   near which the prison was located; fleet ^1 ( def. )

Qu'est-ce que le fleet management ?

   Fleet management : tout savoir Fleet management : tout savoir Fleet
   management : tout savoir Fleet management : tout savoir Fleet
   management : tout savoir Fleet management : tout savoir

   bridgestone fleetcare bridgestone fleetcare white

   Le fleet management (ou gestion de flotte) regroupe les processus
   administratifs d'acquisition, d'entretien, d'organisation et de revente
   des vhicules commerciaux. Elle inclut galement la surveillance en
   temps rel de l'tat des vhicules, de leur position gographique et
   des comportements de conduite des conducteurs. Le fleet management peut
   galement faire rfrence aux composants matriels et logiciels
   ncessaires  la configuration des fonctionnalits de surveillance et
   de golocalisation indispensables  une gestion adquate de votre parc.

Quels sont les avantages du fleet management ?

   Quels sont les avantages du fleet management ?

   Le fleet management est devenu un lment cl pour les entreprises qui
   disposent de vhicules commerciaux. Le principal avantage du fleet
   management est la possibilit de surveiller et d'analyser leur flotte
   dans le dtail.

   Cela permet de nombreuses optimisations trs prcises, qui s'ajoutent
   les unes aux autres et amliorent significativement l'efficacit. Outre
   les avantages conomiques vidents pour le fleet manager, il permet de
   mieux comprendre le fonctionnement de la flotte dans son ensemble. Il
   vous permettra notamment d'optimiser vos cots de carburant ou le temps
   de vos chauffeurs grce aux donnes remontes. Grce  ces
   informations, les dirigeants de l'entreprise sont en mesure de prendre
   de meilleures dcisions, avec davantage de transparence.

Technologie de fleet management

   Technologie de fleet management

   Le fleet management inclut une large gamme de composants matriels et
   logiciels, qui permettent de mesurer, d'enregistrer, de traiter et de
   transmettre  un serveur d'importants volumes de donnes pertinentes en
   temps rel et d'en faciliter la gestion pour le fleet manager. Ces
   composants comprennent notamment :
     * Un systme de golocalisation par GPS dans chaque vhicule de votre
       flotte ;
     * Divers appareils de surveillance, qui enregistrent les vnements
       de conduite tels que la vitesse excessive, les acclrations et
       freinages brusques ainsi que des alertes sur l'tat de chaque
       vhicule ;
     * Un systme permettant aux appareils de se connecter au rseau
       cellulaire ou satellite ;
     * Un serveur, qui traite toutes les donnes et les renvoie aux
       appareils et applications de fleet management connects ;
     * Un logiciel de fleet management pour l'utilisateur final (fleet
       manager ou gestionnaire de flotte), qui affiche toutes ces
       informations de manire claire.

   En s'appuyant sur cette association troite de diffrentes
   technologies, le logiciel de fleet management est capable d'afficher
   des donnes en temps rel sur l'ensemble de la flotte.

Le fleet management, une solution qui s'adapte  toutes les entreprises

   Le fleet management, une solution qui s'adapte  toutes les entreprises

   De plus en plus d'entreprises de transport, notamment les entreprises
   des secteurs du transport par poids lourd, avion ou bateau et de la
   livraison, adoptent le fleet management pour grer leur flotte et
   dmatrialiser encore davantage leur approche des oprations  grande
   chelle. L'un des principaux aspects du fleet management est sa
   capacit  s'adapter  toutes les entreprises, quelle que soit leur
   taille. En outre, la rentabilit de ce systme augmente avec l'chelle
   de mise en oeuvre.

   Les logiciels de fleet management vous permettent de visualiser la
   position physique et l'tat de tous les vhicules d'une flotte ainsi
   que le comportement des conducteurs d'un simple coup d'oeil. Vous
   pouvez facilement et rapidement obtenir des informations sur un
   vhicule donn, et ce  tout moment. Les oprations  grande chelle
   tireront le meilleur parti d'une solution de fleet management.
   Toutefois, cette dernire peut galement amliorer considrablement
   l'efficacit d'entreprises plus petites.

   Prfrences en matire de cookies

   Lorsque vous naviguez sur notre site Web, nous sommes susceptibles
   d'utiliser des cookies et des technologies similaires pour stocker ou
   rcuprer des donnes, dont certaines peuvent tre de nature
   personnelle.

   Pour amliorer votre exprience, nous vous demandons votre consentement
   pour activer d'autres catgories de cookies. Ces catgories incluent la
   publicit cible, la personnalisation et l'analyse. En activant ces
   catgories, vous nous permettez de vous offrir une exprience
   utilisateur optimale.

   intrts.

   interactions des visiteurs et de fournir des contenus spcialement
   adapts  vos besoins.
   [X] Essentiel

   notre site Web et sont traits pour vous fournir le service du site
   Web.

   Vous avez la possibilit d'ajuster vos prfrences en matire de
   cookies  tout moment lors de l'utilisation de nos services Web.
   Veuillez noter que votre consentement est la base juridique du
   traitement des cookies non essentiels.

Fleet management. Business management. Well take care of it.

   Managing your fleet day to day is hard enough. Anticipating the future?
   That seems impossible. But thats exactly how we do it at Enterprise
   Fleet Management. By combining analytics and our client strategy
   managers industry expertise, we keep an eye out for whats on the way
    and how to make the most of it.
   I got with the two salespeople and they put together a fleet analysis,
   showing the entire fleet. Based on the analysis, our local client
   strategy manager recommends rotates the vehicles in and out, even
   though were relatively short, there are 19 vehicles this year, 12
   vehicles next year that we need to retire.
   greg macgraw

   Greg MacGraw, Vice President of Transportation, Cano Health

   Miami, FL

   Reimbursement model vs. Company-Owned Fleet

   Why Companies are Switching from a Reimbursement Model to a
   Company-Owned Fleet
   Download the white paper.

   One of the most fundamental choices you need to make with your fleet
   management strategy is deciding between an employee reimbursement model
   and a company-owned or leased fleet where employees are provided
   vehicles. Download to see five reasons why companies are moving away
   from a reimbursement model for their fleet and get tips on how to get
   employees on board with a company-owned fleet.

   future of transportation. While adoption is still in the early stages,

   fleet needs.

fleet

   5 ENTRIES FOUND:
     * fleet (noun)
     * fleet (adjective)
     * fleet-footed (adjective)
     * fleeting (adjective)
     * Fleet Street (noun)

   ^1 fleet /|fli:t/ noun
   plural fleets
   ^1 fleet
   /|fli:t/
   noun
   plural fleets
   Britannica Dictionary definition of FLEET
   [count]
   1
   a  : a group of military ships that are controlled by one leader
     * He was the commander of the Pacific fleet.

   [+] more examples [-] hide examples [+] Example sentences [-] Hide
   examples
   b  : all of a country's military ships
     * the British fleet

   [+] more examples [-] hide examples [+] Example sentences [-] Hide
   examples
   2
   : a group of ships or vehicles that move or work together or that are
   controlled or owned by one company
     * a fishing fleet
     * The company has a large fleet of delivery trucks.
     * a fleet of taxis

   [+] more examples [-] hide examples [+] Example sentences [-] Hide
   examples
   ^2 fleet /|fli:t/ adjective
   fleeter; fleetest
   ^2 fleet
   /|fli:t/
   adjective
   fleeter; fleetest
   Britannica Dictionary definition of FLEET
   literary
   : very fast
     * a fleet runner
     * She is very fleet of foot. [=she is able to run very fast]

   [+] more examples [-] hide examples [+] Example sentences [-] Hide
   examples

-- fleetly

   adverb
     * running fleetly

   [+] more examples [-] hide examples [+] Example sentences [-] Hide
   examples

-- fleetness

   noun [noncount]
     * fleetness of foot

   [+] more examples [-] hide examples [+] Example sentences [-] Hide
   examples

What is Personal Contract Hire?

   Personal Contract Hire (PCH) is a long-term leasing agreement that
   allows you to drive a brand new or nearly new vehicle over an agreed
   period and mileage.
   (BUTTON) Read More

What is Business Contract Hire?

   Business Contract Hire (BCH) is a long-term leasing agreement that
   allows you to drive a brand-new vehicle over an agreed period and
   mileage.

fleet

   1 of 3

noun

   Synonyms of fleet next
   1
   : a number of warships under a single command
   specifically : an organization of ships and aircraft under the command
   of a flag officer
   2
   : group sense 1
   especially : a group (as of ships, planes, or trucks) operated under
   unified control

   fleet
   2 of 3

adjective

   1
   : swift in motion : nimble
   2
   : fleeting
   fleetly adverb
   fleetness noun

   fleet
   3 of 3

verb

   flt  How to pronounce fleet (audio)
   fleeted; fleeting; fleets

   intransitive verb
   1
   a
   : to fade away : vanish
   b
   archaic : flow
   2
   [fleet entry 2] : to fly swiftly
   clouds fleeting across the sky
   3
   obsolete : drift

   transitive verb
   : to cause (time) to pass usually quickly or imperceptibly
    many young gentlemen  fleet the time carelessly -- William
   Shakespeare

Synonyms of fleet

   Relevance

   Noun
     * convoy
     * armada
     * caravan
     * flotilla
     * parade
     * train

   Adjective
     * rapid
     * brisk
     * fast
     * quick
     * galloping
     * swift
     * flying

   See All Synonyms &amp; Antonyms in Thesaurus
   Choose the Right Synonym for fleet

   fast, rapid, swift, fleet, quick, speedy, hasty, expeditious mean
   moving, proceeding, or acting with celerity.

   fast and rapid are very close in meaning, but fast applies particularly
   to the thing that moves
   fast horses

   and rapid to the movement itself.
   rapid current

   swift suggests great rapidity coupled with ease of movement.
   returned the ball with one swift stroke

   fleet adds the implication of lightness and nimbleness.
   fleet runners

   quick suggests promptness and the taking of little time.
   a quick wit

   speedy implies quickness of successful accomplishment
   speedy delivery of mail

   and may also suggest unusual velocity.

   hasty suggests hurry and precipitousness and often connotes
   carelessness.
   a hasty inspection

   expeditious suggests efficiency together with rapidity of
   accomplishment.
   the expeditious handling of an order

Examples of fleet in a Sentence

   Noun He was the commander of the Pacific fleet. The company has a large
   fleet of delivery trucks. Adjective a jewel thief said to be light of
   heart and fleet of foot
   Recent Examples on the Web
   Examples are automatically compiled from online sources to show current
   usage. Read More Opinions expressed in the examples do not represent
   those of Merriam-Webster or its editors. Send us feedback.
   Noun
   Russia is in second place, with half its fleet, and China is in third
   place. -- Ameya Paleja, Interesting Engineering, 29 Dec. 2025 Their
   wide array of vehicles in the fleet are comfortable, rightly priced and
   deliver reliability and exterior aesthetics all can enjoy. -- Marc D.
   Grasso, Boston Herald, 25 Dec. 2025
   Adjective
   Their captain commander was a good-hearted man, God have mercy on his
   soul, and alongside an affable man like him, the job of the fleet
   master was to crack the whip. -- Literary Hub, 4 Dec. 2025 During that
   time, Chinese shipyards have been turning out warships at frenetic
   pace, the nation surpassing the US in fleet size several years ago. --
   Brad Lendon, CNN Money, 26 Nov. 2025
   Verb
   And Briney conveyed so much with his eyes, fleeting emotional flickers
   that told us everything his character couldnt bring himself to say. --
   Malala Yousafzai, Entertainment Weekly, 10 Dec. 2025 Zwicky, which
   scans the night sky with a wide-field camera, has a reputation of
   enabling astronomers to discover transients or fleeting cosmic
   phenomena, such as quickly flaring supernovae. -- Ashley Strickland,
   CNN Money, 7 Nov. 2025 See All Example Sentences for fleet

Word History

   Etymology

   Noun

   Middle English flete, from Old English flot ship, from flotan

   Adjective

   probably from fleet entry 3

   Verb

   Middle English fleten, from Old English flotan; akin to Old High
   German fliozzan to float, Old English flwan to flow
   First Known Use

   Noun

   13th century, in the meaning defined at sense 1

   Adjective

   circa 1529, in the meaning defined at sense 1

   Verb

   before the 12th century, in the meaning defined at intransitive sense 3
   Time Traveler
   The first known use of fleet was before the 12th century
   See more words from the same century

FAQs

   What is a Fleet Management Solution?

   A fleet management solution brings together telematics and fleet
   management software so that organisations can better oversee their
   vehicles and increase efficiency, cost control, and safety through
   vehicle tracking, driver behaviour monitoring, maintenance scheduling,
   compliance support, reporting, and data analysis.

   What is Fleet Telematics?

   Fleet telematics is a technology used to manage vehicle fleets through
   real-time data collection by combining GPS with onboard diagnostics,
   continuously monitoring location, speed, fuel consumption, driving
   habits, and various other operational details to help fleet managers
   make informed decisions.

   What are the Benefits of a Fleet Telematics System?

   A fleet telematics system offers a wide range of advantages by
   delivering detailed performance metrics. Specifically, managers can
   streamline routes, cut down on fuel costs, and stay on top of
   maintenance needs before issues arise. Driver safety is likewise
   improved through monitoring and feedback, and accurate tracking
   enhances customer service with precise ETAs.

   Is Fleet Telematics the Same as Fleet Tracking?

   No, fleet tracking primarily focuses on the location of vehicles.
   Telematics, on the other hand, offers a comprehensive view that
   includes vehicle performance, driver behaviour, fuel usage, and
   maintenance needs. Therefore, tracking tells you where your vehicles
   are, and telematics explains how they're being driven in order to
   maintain a safer, more compliant, and cost-effective fleet.

   Which Industries use Fleet Management Solutions?

   Regardless of a business's or company's size, fleet management
   solutions are in use within a wide range of sectors, including
   transport and logistics, courier and delivery, public transportation,
   cold chain, construction, as well as waste and recycling.

Fleet

Distinctive literary publishing


   Fleet was launched in 2016 as a home for great literary fiction and
   non-fiction, publishing eight to ten titles a year. Dynamic,
   forward-thinking and focused on bringing the very best books to
   readers, in the short time that Fleet has been running our books have
   made a huge impact: fiction from literary powerhouses such as Clare
   Messud, Virginia Baily, Paula McLean and Colson Whitehead as well as
   affecting and important non-fiction from authors including Hope Jahren,
   Michelle Dean and Ariel Levy. Now a well-established list, we at Fleet
   are also keen to search out the new voices of the future, including
   Yara Rodrigues Fowler, Adelle Stripe, Samas O'Reilly and Ronan Farrow.

Welcome to Fleet Town Council

   Our vision for Fleet "A welcoming, sustainable inclusive and
   environmentally ambitious town, looking to the future with a strong,
   healthy, active and diverse community which responds positively to
   change."
   Read more about our vision

   Image of a deer in the woodland at Oakley park

About Fleet

   Regularly voted as one of the most desirable places to live in the UK,
   Fleet is a warm and welcoming town which truly has a community spirit
   at its heart. Fleet is the perfect destination for an evening out -
   with a choice of independent restaurants and entertainment in the form
   of our very own venue, The Harlington. Alternatively, take a
   picturesque stroll around Fleet Pond, along Basingstoke Canal or one of
   our award-winning Parks.

Building a Better Community Together

   Your Community is our Priority

   Serving the Community to Build a Brighter Future

   Here you'll find information about us and the services we provide.
   Fleet Town Council is committed to assisting residents with local
   issues - we're here to help, advise or point anyone with a problem in
   the right direction, we're only a phone call or email away.

   Fleet manager
     __________________________________________________________________

If you've received a traffic violation notice for the vehicle you use for
professional purposes,
DO NOT pay your fine straight away!


   Fleet managers are subject to the same designation requirements
   applicable to any legal representative of a legal entity.

   Article L.121-6 of the French Highway Code stipulates that when an
   offence is committed with a vehicle registered in the name of the legal
   entity, its legal representative is required to designate the driver at
   the time the offence was recorded. If they do not do so within 45 days,
   they will receive a notice of failure to designate, in addition to the
   initial traffic violation notice.


   ANTAI offers fleet managers three solutions to simplify the process

SMALL FLEETS (up to 10 vehicles) : personal designation


   If you are fleet manager for 1 to 10 vehicles, designation can be done
   on a personal basis, using the same procedure as self-employed or
   private individuals.


   Click on the following button to access the personal designation
   procedure

   PERSONAL DESIGNATION


   You must select option No. 2 "I am self-employed " and designate the
   driver. Make sure you have the traffic violation notice you received,
   as well as the name, contact details, and driving licence details of
   the person who was driving the vehicle when the offence was committed.

   Fleet managers can also carry out the designation by sending the
   request for exemption form (which is attached to the traffic violation
   notice received) and all necessary supporting documents by registered
   post with acknowledgement of receipt.

MEDIUM FLEETS (more than 10 vehicles) : there is a special section for
designation


   The special section for fleet managers is intended for fleets of more
   than 10 vehicles. It allows you to designate several drivers
   simultaneously, modify or delete a designation, and access a record of
   all your procedures.

   This special section for professionals has recently been redesigned
   with a more ergonomic interface to make it more user-friendly.


   You first need to create an account to access it

   Create a fleet manager account


   To save time, make sure you have the following documents available:


       o The traffic violation notice addressed to the legal entity and
   issued within the last month

       o The company registration certificate or proof of registration in
   the French trade and
   companies register

       o A certificate confirming the legal representative has delegation
   to use the service

LARGE FLEETS (over 1000 vehicles) : sign an agreement with ANTAI


   Companies with a fleet of more than 1,000 vehicles can sign an
   agreement with ANTAI and benefit from automated procedures,
   particularly for the exchange of information required to designate
   drivers.

   This exchange of information means the traffic violation notice is sent
   directly to the driver and not to the legal representative of the legal
   entity, which saves you time and reduces the risk of mistakes.


   If you'd like to sign an agreement with ANTAI, you'll first need to
   create an account for your company

   Create my company account


   To allow us to process your request, don't forget to attach a company
   registration certificate proving the legal existence of your company.

   <lt;- Logistics Dictionary

Fleet Vehicles

What are Fleet Vehicles?

   Fleet vehicles comprise of all the transport vehicles owned by a
   company, government agency or other business. Sometimes, the vehicles
   are leased to the transport companies for the movement of goods to
   customer. In other case, the vehicles are leased to get a company's
   employees (particularly the sales representatives) to their clients
   location.

   If the vehicles are owned by private employees and they are primarily
   used for work related activities, this type of fleet is called the
   "grey fleet".
   The series of vehicles owned and managed by a company or government
   organisation.

   DG MARE

Fleet Register

   The EU Fleet Register is a database where all the fishing vessels
   flying the flag of an EU country have to be be registered. Any changes
   in the status of a fishing vessel, for example if it has been scrapped,
   need to be registered by the member country in the Fleet Register.

   The main objective of the Fleet Register is to enable any EU fishing
   vessel with key characteristics to be identified, with the aim of:
     * Monitoring the implementation of capacity management countries
     * Being a source of information for the officials of the European
       Commission and EU countries in charge of control and inspection
     * Serving as an accurate source of statistical data on the European
       fishing fleet
     * Serving as a reference database for vessels characteristics for
       other applications that manage information on fishing vessels

   The data contained in the Fleet Register can be classified in three
   categories:
     * Administrative identifications Vessel name, registration port,
       external marking, Unique Vessel Identifier number (IMO number),
       International RadioCallSign, etc.
     * Technical characteristics Length, tonnage, power, fishing gear,
       etc.
     * Historical events Entry into and exit from the fleet, modifications
       of characteristics

   Loading...
   Fleet Register 1.2.0.1
   This site is managed by the Directorate-General for Maritime Affairs
   and Fisheries

   From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia
   Look up fleet in Wiktionary, the free dictionary.

   Fleet may refer to:

Vehicles

   [edit]
     * Fishing fleet
     * Naval fleet
     * Fleet vehicles, a pool of motor vehicles
     * Fleet Aircraft, the aircraft manufacturing company

Places

   [edit]

Canada

   [edit]
     * Fleet, Alberta, Canada, a hamlet

England

   [edit]
     * Fleet (Kent), a term for a waterway in the Thames marshes, England
     * The Fleet (Tees), a section of the River Tees' original route
     * Fleet, Dorset, England, a village and civil parish
     * Fleet, Hampshire, England, a town and civil parish
     * Fleet, Hayling Island, Hampshire, England, a hamlet
     * Fleet, Lincolnshire, England
     * The Fleet Lagoon, at Chesil Beach, Dorset
     * Fleet Pond, Hampshire, England
     * River Fleet, subterranean river in London, England
          + Fleet Street, named after the river
          + Fleet Prison, named after the river
          + Fleet Line, named after the river, was the original name for
            the London Underground Jubilee line

Scotland

   [edit]
     * Fleet Bay, a part of a National Scenic Area within Dumfries and
       Galloway, Scotland
     * Water of Fleet, a river in Scotland

United States

   [edit]
     * Fleet, Kentucky, US, an unincorporated community

In business

   [edit]
     * ARC Centre of Excellence in Future Low-Energy Electronics
       Technologies (aka FLEET), Australian physics research collaboration
       developing ultra-low-energy electronics
     * Blain's Farm &amp; Fleet, American farm and ranch supply store chain
     * Fleet (laxative), brand name for Bisacodyl
     * Fleet Aircraft, a Canadian manufacturer of aircraft from 1928 to
       1957
     * FleetBoston Financial, a financial institution also known as Fleet
       Bank
     * Fleet Farm, American farm and ranch supply store chain
     * Fleet services, an English motorway service station
     * Fleet Space Technologies, an Australian company led by Flavia Tata
       Nardini

People

   [edit]
     * Fleet (surname), includes a list of people with the name
     * Moses Fleetwood Walker (1856-1924), nicknamed "Fleet", American
       baseball player

Other uses

   [edit]
     * Ebbsfleet United F.C., an English football team whose nickname is
       "The Fleet"
     * The Fleet series of shared universe science fiction anthologies
       edited by David Drake and Bill Fawcett
     * Fleet (horse)
     * Fleets, a discontinued disappearing messaging feature of the
       Twitter platform
     * Wilmington Clippers (1937-1950), an American football team
       nicknamed "The Fleet"
     * Fleet, an IDE developed by JetBrains
     * Boston Fleet, a women's hockey team

People. Performance. Partnership.

With a proud 30-year history, we are meticulous custodians of your vessels
and cargo.

   Welcome

World-Class Fleet Management Services

   With the support of our teams of global professionals, we are shaping
   the future of ship management, ensuring that the goods and materials
   powering global economies are delivered swiftly and securely to their
   destinations.

   We are custodians of our clients' vessels and cargo, looking after them
   as if they were our own. A combination of experience, reliability,
   innovation and sheer passion have enabled us to become one of the
   world's leading providers of ship management services.

   Three main attributes form the core of our business: an unparalleled
   commitment to our people both onshore and at sea, client partnerships
   led by integrity and transparency, and a commitment to building the
   most innovative ship management company in the world.

People: Empowering Our Global Workforce

   Seafaring is in our DNA. From our crews to our Chairman, maritime trade
   is what drives us. It is a passion shared by our commitment to the
   27,000 seafarers and 1,200 shore-based colleagues we have working
   across 24 offices in 10 countries. Their commitment to excellence and
   dedication to their roles are the foundation of the work we do for our
   clients.
   Our commitment to transparent partnerships, alongside operational
   insights and a deep passion for protecting their assets which allows us
   to foster enduring relationships that thrive on shared goals and help
   shape the future of global maritime operations.
   We are investing heavily to equip the next-generation of seafarers with
   the skills and knowledge to excel in tomorrow's maritime industry. From
   sea to shore, we integrate the wellbeing and professional development
   of our teams into every aspect of our operations, ensuring that vessels
   operate seamlessly and meet our clients' evolving needs.
   Our talent development programmes provide comprehensive training,
   education, and mentoring opportunities, to equip our seafarers and
   employees with the skills needed to navigate an industry marked by
   digitalisation, automation, and regulatory requirements.
   Fleet Care also offers best-in-class seafarer wellbeing programmes,
   including family-centred benefits, mental health support from on-staff
   psychologists, and other resources that ensure our teams are fully
   supported.

Partnerships: Tailored Solutions for Long-Term Success

   We recognise that our role now extends beyond traditional ship
   management; our clients need partners and innovators as we navigate
   increasingly complex industry challenges together.  We are partners in
   your growth and success. Our commitment to clients is anchored in
   integrity, with a long-term view of relationships and investments. This
   focus ensures optimal fleet performance and the flexibility to adapt to
   industry shifts. Many of our client partnerships span decades, built on
   mutual trust, transparent communication, and the ability to navigate
   global challenges together.
   The size of our operations - with more than 650+ ships under Technical
   Management and New Building Supervision services - provides us with the
   insight to identify trends early, enabling us to offer our clients
   fresh perspectives and solutions that anticipate emerging needs. Many
   of our client partnerships span decades, built on mutual trust,
   transparent communication, and the ability to navigate global
   challenges together.
   As the maritime industry evolves, we remain committed to helping our
   clients transition toward a zero-carbon future. By working hand-in-hand
   with partners, we integrate sustainable practices and future-focused
   strategies, ensuring that together we can navigate towards a more
   sustainable shipping industry.

Performance: Driving Global Economic Growth with Operational Excellence

   With a 240+ strong technical and operations team, we maintain vessels
   to the highest standards, ensuring compliance and operational
   excellence. From routine maintenance to managing complex technical
   challenges, we handle every aspect of vessel operations to ensure ships
   remain compliant with international regulations. Assisting vessels
   around the world, our 80+ QHSE (Quality, Health, Safety, and
   Environment) professionals help our crews to uphold the highest safety
   standards.
   Our focus on proactive maintenance helps reduce downtime while
   increasing efficiency and reliability, and delivering the performance
   our clients expect. With strategically positioned technical and crewing
   hubs, we deliver round-the-clock support. This combination of global
   scale and local expertise allows us to respond quickly and effectively
   to client needs, ensuring that vessels operate smoothly, no matter
   where they are in the world.
   Every vessel we manage plays a crucial role in transporting essential
   goods and raw materials that sustain industries worldwide. Whether it's
   energy, agriculture, or consumer goods, we play our part in ensuring
   supply chains remain uninterrupted, helping businesses and communities
   thrive in today's interconnected global economy.

   tanker SAMC SWAN (IMO 9813058) confirm that the vessel was attacked at
   approximately 1600 HKT on 20thNovember when the 8708-dwt vessel was on
   passage in the Southern Red Sea region.

   All non essential crew promptly mustered in the ships citadel.
   Anti-piracy procedures were immediately carried out.

   All relevant authorities including the UKMTO and IMB Piracy Centre have
   been informed. Fleet has assembled its emergency response team and is
   taking all measures to safeguard the crew.

Welcome to your Fleet Science Center

   The Fleet Science Center is on a mission to realize a San Diego where
   everyone is connected by the power of science to create a better
   future. We are a community-focused countywide organization that
   collaborates with educators, scientific organizations, community
   leaders and champions of a-ha moments. Join us and explore a world of
   possibility!

Know Before You Go

     * Hours &amp; Admission
     * Directions
     * Parking
     * Calendar

   Get Tickets

   Explore STEM in San Diego County with the Fleet Science Center. It all
   starts with a moment in place of inspiration. These spots mark
   education programs, collaborations with community partners and events
   to foster engagement, learning and meaningful connections between our
   organization and the communities we serve. Use this map to discover new
   opportunities to connect with the power of science in your
   neighborhood.

   The Fleet is San Diego's science center, bringing together the
   community and offering meaningful experiences that embrace the power of
   science and impact the world around us.

Factsheet

What does the verb fleet mean?

   There are 20 meanings listed in OED's entry for the verb fleet, 12 of
   which are labelled obsolete. See `Meaning &amp; use' for definitions,
   usage, and quotation evidence.

   fleet has developed meanings and uses in subjects including
   nautical (Old English) fish (Old English)

Entry status

   OED is undergoing a continuous programme of revision to modernize and
   improve definitions. This entry has not yet been fully revised.
   See meaning &amp; use

What is the etymology of the verb fleet?

   fleet is a word inherited from Germanic.
   See etymology

What is the earliest known use of the verb fleet?

   Earliest known use

   Old English

   The earliest known use of the verb fleet is in the Old English period
   (pre-1150).

   It is also recorded as a noun from the Old English period (pre-1150).
   See meaning &amp; use

How is the verb fleet pronounced?

British English

   /fli:t/
   fleet
   (BUTTON) Listen to pronunciation

U.S. English

   /flit/
   fleet
   (BUTTON) Listen to pronunciation
   See pronunciation

Etymology

Summary

   A word inherited from Germanic.
   A Common Germanic originally strong verb; Old English flotan (flat,
   fluton, floten) to float, corresponding to Old Frisian fliata, Old
   Saxon fliotan (Middle Dutch, Dutch vlieten) to flow...    (BUTTON) Show
   more
   A Common Germanic originally strong verb; Old English flotan (flat,
   fluton, floten) to float, corresponding to Old Frisian fliata, Old
   Saxon fliotan (Middle Dutch, Dutch vlieten) to flow, Old High German
   flioan to float, flow (Middle High German vlieen, modern German
   flieen to flow), Old Norse flita (Swedish flyta, Danish flde) to
   float, flow (not recorded in Gothic) <lt; Old Germanic *fleutan (flaut,
   flutum, flotono-), <lt; pre-Germanic root *pleud-, ploud-, plud- (compare
   Latvian pludinat to float, pludot to flow, pludi flood, Lithuanian
   plsti to float away, pludis float of a fishing-net), an extended form
   of the Old Aryan root *pleu-, plu- (compare Greek plen to sail,
   Sanskrit plu, pru to swim, float, flow, Latin pluere to rain.
   (BUTTON) Show less
   (BUTTON) Cite

              1. fleetOld English-1590
                 intransitive. To drift or be carried by the current or
                 tide on the surface of the water. Obsolete.
              2. to-fleetOld English-1230
                 intransitive. To float away; to be carried away by or as
                 by water.

                 figurative.

                 transported on flowing water. Also figurative.

                 favouring tides, and anchoring when the tide...

         5. II.11.

            verbs] move swiftly
              1. flyOld English-
                 intransitive. To move through the air with wings. Also
                 with adverbs, as about, away, forth, off, out, etc. as
                 the crow flies: see crow, n. 3c.
              2. lakeOld English-1540
                 intransitive. To exert oneself, move quickly, leap,
                 spring; hence, to fight. Obsolete.
              3. runOld English-
                 intransitive. In various figurative contexts, generally
                 with the implication of rapid motion.
              4. hiec1330-1607
                 To advance or come on quickly, hasten on; to `get on',
                 make progress; to speed, prosper.
              5. jace1393
                 Alteration of chace, apparently for alliteration's sake.
              6. scoura1400-
                 To move rapidly, go in haste, run. Chiefly with adverbs,
                 indicating the direction, etc.
              7. spina1400-
                 To move rapidly; to run quickly; now esp. to ride or
                 drive at a rapid and even rate.
              8. fleetc1400-
                 intransitive. To move swiftly; to flit, fly. Also with
                 away. Cf. fleet, adj.
              9. glentc1400-
                 Of light, etc.: To be reflected, flash, gleam, etc. Of
                 luminous or reflecting objects: To shine. (Cf. glint, v.
                 i.) Now only dialect.
             10. stripc1400-1616
                 intransitive. To move or pass swiftly.

                 intransitive. To move, go, esp. quickly; to sally forth
                 or out. Cf. sway, v. I.1a. Obsolete.
             12. carryc1450-1568
                 intransitive. To go or travel vigorously or speedily; to
                 proceed rapidly. Chiefly with adverb or prepositional
                 phrase indicating direction. Obsolete.
             13. speed1488-
                 intransitive. To go or move with speed. Also with it, up.

                 dart nimbly from place to place.

    3. III.
       Nautical uses.
         1. III.12.
            1769-
            Nautical. transitive. To change the position of, shift (a
            block, rope, etc.). Also absol. [Substituted for the earlier
            flit v. 1d owing probably to association with sense II.10.]
              1. 1769

     To fleet it, or replace in a proper state of action... The man who
     performs this office..calls out, fleet, jigger!
                 W. Falconer, An Universal Dictionary of the Marine at
                 Jigger (BUTTON) Citation details for W. Falconer, An
                 Universal Dictionary of the Marine
              2. 1859

     To fleet blocks is to bring them as close together as possible.
                 F. A. Griffiths, Artillerist's Manual (1862) 107 (BUTTON)
                 Citation details for F. A. Griffiths, Artillerist's
                 Manual
              3. 1867

     Fleeting, the act of changing the situation of a tackle when the
     blocks are drawn together; also, changing the position of the
     dead-eyes, when the shrouds are become too long..Fleet ho! the order
     given at such times.
                 W. H. Smyth &amp; E. Belcher, Sailor's Word-book (BUTTON)
                 Citation details for W. H. Smyth &amp;amp; E. Belcher,
                 Sailor's Word-book
              4. 1867

     Fleet the messenger, when about to weigh, to shift the eyes of the
     messenger past the capstan for the heavy heave.
                 W. H. Smyth &amp; E. Belcher, Sailor's Word-book (BUTTON)
                 Citation details for W. H. Smyth &amp;amp; E. Belcher,
                 Sailor's Word-book
              5. 1882

     Fleet the purchase down to the water's edge.
                 G. S. Nares, Seamanship (ed. 6) 61 (BUTTON) Citation
                 details for G. S. Nares, Seamanship

Forms

Variant forms

   infinitive Old English flotan, (3rd person present tense. flt),
   Middle English fleoten, (Middle English southern vleoten, wleoten),
   Middle English fleote, Middle English-1500s flet(e(n, Middle
   English-1600s fleete, Scottish fleit, Middle English- fleet. past tense
   Old English flat, Middle English Orm. flt, Middle English fleet,
   flote, Middle English-1500s flet, plural Old English fluton, Middle
   English fluten, floten; weak forms Middle English fletide, Middle
   English-1500s flette, 1500s Scottish fletit, fletted, 1600s fle(e)ted.
   past participle Old English, Middle English floten (see flotten adj.).

Frequency

   fleet typically occurs about three times per million words in modern
   written English.
   Frequency data is computed programmatically, and should be regarded as
   an estimate.

Frequency of fleet, v., 1750-2010

   * Occurrences per million words in written English

   Historical frequency series are derived from Google Books Ngrams
   (version 2), a data set based on the Google Books corpus of several
   million books printed in English between 1500 and 2010.

   The overall frequency for a given word is calculated by summing
   frequencies for the main form of the word, any plural or inflected
   forms, and any major spelling variations.

   For sets of homographs (distinct entries that share the same word-form,
   e.g. mole, n., mole, n., mole, n., etc.), we have estimated the
   frequency of each homograph entry as a fraction of the total Ngrams
   frequency for the word-form. This may result in inaccuracies.

   Smoothing has been applied to series for lower-frequency words, using a
   moving-average algorithm. This reduces short-term fluctuations, which
   may be produced by variability in the content of the Google Books
   corpus.

What is fleet management?

   Group of vehicles driving

Authors

   Tom Krantz

   Staff Writer

   IBM Think
   Alexandra Jonker

   Staff Editor

   IBM Think

What is fleet management?

   Fleet management encompasses the systems, processes and tools needed to
   ensure an organization's fleet of business vehicles are maintained
   throughout their entire lifecycle.

   It also involves the management of fleet drivers, optimization of fleet
   operations, and improvement of asset utilization--all of which fall
   under the larger umbrella of enterprise asset management.

   A fleet can range from commercial vehicles to freight trucks to
   airplanes, depending on the organization. Some organizations may build
   their own fleet while others may hire fleet management companies. With
   proper fleet management, organizations can improve efficiency, reduce
   costs and ensure that their business is complying with the latest
   regulations.
   3D design of balls rolling on a track

The latest AI News + Insights

   Discover expertly curated insights and news on AI, cloud and more in
   the weekly Think Newsletter.
   (BUTTON) Subscribe today

What are the benefits of fleet management?

   A smart fleet management system can help organizations achieve their
   environmental, social and governance (ESG) goals. For instance, fleet
   providers can play a crucial role in minimizing their organization's
   economic footprint by tracking fuel consumption and other diagnostic
   data. In doing so, they can also improve fleet performance by
   preventing vehicles from breaking down and avoiding costly vehicle
   acquisitions.

   Fleet management can also enable more positive vehicle inspection
   outcomes. A poor vehicle inspection can ground vehicles, even when they
   are fully operational. Fleet management can provide either the
   predictive or preventative maintenance that keeps vehicles in
   circulation.

   Finally, by implementing various fleet management solutions, companies
   can improve upkeep, avoid last-minute cancellations and increase the
   number of customers they serve. This can result in more satisfied
   customers and greater profitability.
   Mixture of Experts | 2 January, episode 88

Decoding AI: Weekly News Roundup

   Join our world-class panel of engineers, researchers, product leaders
   and more as they cut through the AI noise to bring you the latest in AI
   news and insights.
   Watch all episodes of Mixture of Experts

What does a fleet manager do?

   Fleet managers oversee fleet operations while maintaining fleet
   efficiency. At a glance, that may seem like routine asset performance
   management or fleet maintenance. However, a fleet manager's
   responsibilities include everything from:

Acquiring vehicles

   Fleet managers determine when a new vehicle is needed, what
   requirements the vehicle must meet, and whether an older vehicle needs
   to be replaced.

Maintaining the fleet

   Fleet managers are responsible for preventative vehicle maintenance to
   avoid breakdowns, balancing maintenance costs with maintenance
   schedules and handling repairs so the fleet remains operational.

Managing drivers

   Fleet managers recruit drivers, manage their workflows and work to
   retain talent. They also ensure driver safety by implementing proper
   training, monitoring driver behavior and optimizing safety procedures.

Implementing fuel programs

   Fleet managers are responsible for fuel management, which includes
   mapping out preferred fueling locations, creating processes to track
   fuel usage and lowering fueling costs for the entire vehicle fleet.

Complying with regulations

   Fleet managers oversee hours of service (HOS), electronic logging
   devices (ELDs) and other regulatory requirements to ensure that all
   drivers are operating in full compliance.

Reducing operational costs

   Fleet managers compare fleet costs, aggregate data in a central
   location and perform route optimization to improve cost savings and
   reduce downtime. Many use fleet management solutions or fleet
   management software to automate and optimize these tasks.

What is fleet management software?

   Fleet management software provides fleet managers with a real-time
   logistics platform that can orchestrate everything from fleet tracking
   and driver management to weather and traffic forecasting. Fleet
   management solutions can also improve fuel usage, provide key vehicle
   metrics and fleet data, ensure compliance with regulatory requirements
   and ultimately, optimize fleet operations.

   While similar to a transportation management system that allows a
   company to manage its inbound and outbound shipping, fleet management
   software differs in that it's more robust and focuses solely on the
   commercial fleet.

   Some common features of fleet management software include:
     * Vehicle tracking: With GPS and OEM telematics, fleet managers are
       gaining real-time visibility into their fleet while it's on the
       road. They can monitor vehicle locations, analyze driver
       performance and mitigate potential maintenance issues through
       real-time notifications. The ability to pinpoint a vehicle through
       asset tracking is a defining feature of a good fixed asset
       management strategy.

     * Driving behavior analysis: Often, vehicle tracking systems have an
       additional driving behavior component that assigns drivers with a
       score or rating. For example, frequent braking or idling can impact
       scores negatively. Some organizations may also use dash cams in the
       cabin of their vehicles to keep an eye on their commercial fleet.

     * Fuel management: Fuel efficiency can lead to significant cost
       reductions. Fleet management software monitors fuel costs and track
       vehicles' fuel consumption. It may also automate the refueling
       process to avoid unnecessary refueling or worse, running out of
       fuel.

     * Route planning: Fleet managers will often plan routes in advance
       for optimal efficiency. Through a combination of GPS tracking and
       telematics, fleet managers can precisely map routes, reducing fuel
       costs while improving sustainability efforts.

     * Software updates: The rise of electric vehicles means that fleet
       operators may be expected to have a comprehensive plan to manage
       updates to vehicle operating systems, including when to update
       software and how to roll out the updates.

Where to start with fleet management

   Organizations looking to stand up a strong fleet management program
   should consider these factors:
   Pricing

   A forward-facing fleet management strategy requires significant
   investment out of the gate. Some initial costs include adding
   telematics to vehicles, purchasing or licensing software and reskilling
   the workforce.
   Fleet complexity

   Fleet sizes vary and a fleet of vehicles can range from e-bikes to
   airplanes. Large fleets with a wide variety of vehicles will require
   different approaches to each type of vehicle, especially when it comes
   to preventative maintenance.
   Technology management

   As organizations move towards a more advanced and software-guided fleet
   management approach, they'll need to stay current on the latest
   technologies, update and replace hardware and otherwise manage their
   tech stack.

   provide the highest level of customer service, we arm our customers

   decisions about all aspects of car &amp; van leasing.

   if you need advice we will do our utmost to ensure you receive the best
   and most relevant information. Our dedicated Account Manager team are
   on hand to answer any questions from choosing a vehicle all the way
   through to the end of its contract and beyond.

   contact us.

   DocumentationAmazon EC2User Guide
   Features and benefits

EC2 Fleet and Spot Fleet

   EC2 Fleet and Spot Fleet are designed to be a useful way to launch a
   fleet of tens, hundreds, or thousands of Amazon EC2 instances in a
   single operation. Each instance in a fleet is either configured by a
   launch template or a set of launch parameters that you configure
   manually at launch.

Topics

     * Features and benefits
     * Which is the best fleet method to use?
     * Configuration options for your EC2 Fleet or Spot Fleet
     * Work with EC2 Fleet
     * Work with Spot Fleet
     * Monitor your EC2 Fleet or Spot Fleet
     * Tutorials for EC2 Fleet
     * Example CLI configurations for EC2 Fleet
     * Example CLI configurations Spot Fleet
     * Quotas for EC2 Fleet and Spot Fleet

Features and benefits

   Fleets provide the following features and benefits, enabling you to
   maximize cost savings and optimize availability and performance when
   running applications on multiple EC2 instances.

   Multiple instance types
          A fleet can launch multiple instance types, ensuring it isn't
          dependent on the availability of any single instance type. This
          increases the overall availability of instances in your fleet.

   Distributing instances across Availability Zones
          A fleet can launch into multiple Availability Zones, enabling
          you to reduce costs and improve availability. If your fleet
          includes Spot Instances, the fleet automatically selects
          Availability Zones based on your preferences regarding price and
          interruptions.

   Multiple purchasing options
          A fleet can launch multiple purchase options (Spot and On-Demand
          Instances), allowing you to optimize costs through Spot Instance
          usage. You can also take advantage of Reserved Instance and
          Savings Plans discounts by using them in conjunction with
          On-Demand Instances in the fleet.

   Automated replacement of Spot Instances
          If your fleet includes Spot Instances, it can automatically
          request replacement Spot capacity if your Spot Instances are
          interrupted. Through Capacity Rebalancing, a fleet can also
          monitor and proactively replace your Spot Instances that are at
          an elevated risk of interruption.

   Reserve On-Demand capacity
          A fleet can use an On-Demand Capacity Reservation to reserve
          On-Demand capacity. A fleet can also include Capacity Blocks for
          ML, allowing you to reserve GPU instances on a future date to
          support short duration machine learning (ML) workloads.

   Warning Javascript is disabled or is unavailable in your browser.

   To use the Amazon Web Services Documentation, Javascript must be
   enabled. Please refer to your browser's Help pages for instructions.
   Document Conventions
   Option 3: Manually connect
   Which fleet method to use?

     * Fleet DNA: Commercial Fleet Vehicle Operating Data

Fleet DNA: Commercial Fleet Vehicle Operating Data

   Fleet DNA logo

   The Fleet DNA database of commercial fleet vehicle operating data helps
   vehicle manufacturers and developers optimize vehicle designs and helps
   fleet managers choose advanced technologies.

   A subset of data shown in this online tool provides data summaries and
   visualizations similar to real-world "genetics" for medium- and
   heavy-duty fleet vehicles, which helps users understand the broad
   operational range of commercial vehicles across vocations and weight
   classes.

   FleetDNA is part of the FleetREDI platform.
     * Delivery Vans Delivery Vans
     * Delivery Trucks Delivery Trucks
     * School Buses School Buses
     * Transit Buses Transit Buses

Delivery Vans

   Photo of a delivery van

   The delivery van category includes data for walk-in and step vans
   ranging in size from class 3 to class 6 with primary vocations of
   delivering parcels, food, linens, or beverages.

   Fleet DNA has 974 days of driving data from 94 delivery vans operating
   in the United States.

   Composite Data

   Data Summary and Visualizations

Delivery Trucks

   Photo of a delivery truck

   The delivery truck category includes data for city delivery, straight
   trucks, stake body vehicles, furniture trucks, rack trucks, and
   beverage delivery trucks ranging in size from class 3 to class 7 with
   primary vocations of delivering parcels, food, linens, beverages, or
   warehouse items.

   Fleet DNA has 553 days of driving data from 36 delivery trucks
   operating in the United States.

School Buses

   Photo of a school bus

   The school bus category includes data for a wide range of school bus
   types, including class A to class D body types sized from class 3 to
   class 6.

   Fleet DNA has 857 days of driving data from 204 school buses operating
   in the United States.

Transit Buses

   Photo of a transit bus

   The transit bus category includes a wide range of data for buses
   operating on shorter-distance public transport bus services, which
   include single- and double-deck buses, articulated buses and midibuses.
   The typical operating weight range for this category is class 7 and 8
   with the primary vocation being mass transit.

   Fleet DNA has 472 days of driving data from 19 transit buses operating
   in the United States.

Bucket Trucks

   Photo of a bucket truck

   The bucket truck category includes data for a wide range of vehicle
   chassis builds that have a powered overhead boom with a bucket
   attachment. The sizes of the vehicles in the database range from class
   3 to class 7 with vocational applications in the utility and
   telecommunications fields.

   Fleet DNA has 283 days of driving data from 20 bucket trucks operating
   in the United States.

   Composite Data

   Data Summary and Visualizations

Service Vans

   Photo of a service van

   The service van category includes data for both service and utility
   vans ranging in size from class 2 to class 3 with vocational
   applications in the utility and telecommunications fields.

   Fleet DNA has 29 days of driving data from 4 service vans operating in
   the United States.

   Composite Data

   Data Summary and Visualizations

   (BUTTON) Show More Charts

Tractors

   Photo of a tractor

   The tractor category includes data for a wide range of vehicle types
   and configurations, including beverage delivery tractors, semis,
   sleeper semis, and refrigerated trucks that each have a removable
   trailer and range in size from class 7 to class 8. Vehicles in this
   category also include a wide range of vocations, such as beverage,
   fuel, and food delivery, and regional and long-hauling activities.

   Fleet DNA has 1,150 days of driving data collected from 70 tractors
   operating in the United States.

   Composite Data

   Data Summary and Visualizations

Refuse Trucks

   Photo of a refuse truck

   The refuse truck category includes data for a wide range of
   refuse-hauling vehicles, including front-, side-, and real-loading
   refuse trucks in addition to dumpster-hauling trucks. The typical
   operating weight range for this category is class 8, and the primary
   vocation is refuse pickup.

   Fleet DNA has 387 days of driving data from 39 refuse trucks operating
   in the United States.

Connect With Clean Cities and Communities

   Clean Cities and Communities is a U.S. Department of Energy partnership
   to foster transportation energy choices nationwide. More than 75
   coalitions work with fleets to deliver objective technical expertise
   based on a unique understanding of local markets. Connect with your
   local coalition.

Tools and Resources

   Use these tools to analyze and sort transportation data.

   FleetREDI: Fleet Research, Energy Data, and Insights
   Access our interactive online portal for commercial vehicle data and
   energy insights.

   DRIVE: Drive-Cycle Rapid Investigation, Visualization, and Evaluation
   Analysis Tool
   Produce drive cycles from real-world vehicle data to cut evaluation and
   analysis time.

   U.S. Department of Energy Fleet DNA Data Summaries
   Access daily duty cycle summaries for every vehicle day within NLR's
   Fleet DNA database using the Livewire Data Platform.

   T3CO: Transportation Technology Total Cost of Ownership
   Explore fast, customizable commercial vehicle total cost of ownership
   analyses.

   Browse our complete list of data and tools.

How To Use the Data

   Use these resources to understand the data and get ideas on how to use
   drive cycle metrics.

How To Contribute Your Data

   You can contribute your fleet's data to FleetREDI's Fleet DNA database
   to advance commercial vehicle technologies. Email us at
   [email protected].

How To Cite the Data

   Publications that use data from Fleet DNA should include a citation
   consistent with the following format:

     "Fleet DNA Project Data." ([YEAR]). NLR. Accessed [DATE]:
     www.nrel.gov/fleetdna

   If you use the data in a publication, please notify Andrew Kotz so we
   can gauge the reach of the data.

Contact

   Contact us at [email protected] for more information.

FAQ  propos de la carte Fleet TotalEnergies

(BUTTON) Qu'est-ce qu'une carte carburant et recharge lectrique pour
professionnel ?

   Une carte carburant et recharge lectrique est un outil de paiement
   spcialement conu pour grer l'ensemble des dpenses lies aux
   dplacements professionnels. Conue pour les entreprises, cette carte
   carburant permet de rgler non seulement l'achat de carburant mais
   aussi la recharge des vhicules lectriques dans un rseau tendu de
   stations et bornes. Elle se prsente sous plusieurs formes, allant de
   solutions classiques  des versions plus modernes intgrant la recharge
   lectrique. Ce dispositif simplifie la gestion administrative en
   centralisant toutes les transactions et en fournissant un reporting
   dtaill. Contrairement aux cartes personnelles, la carte essence offre
   une scurit accrue et un suivi prcis des dpenses, facilitant ainsi
   le contrle budgtaire. En intgrant  la fois le carburant
   traditionnel et l'lectricit pour les vhicules modernes, cette
   solution est parfaitement adapte aux besoins volutifs des entreprises
   soucieuses de matriser leurs cots et de favoriser une mobilit plus
   durable. C'est un alli stratgique pour optimiser la gestion des
   dplacements tout en assurant une transparence totale sur les
   consommations.

(BUTTON) Quels sont les avantages d'une carte carburant et recharge
lectrique pour une entreprise ?

   La carte carburant et recharge lectrique offre de nombreux avantages
   pour les entreprises qui souhaitent optimiser la gestion de leur flotte
   de vhicules. Tout d'abord, elle centralise toutes les transactions
   lies  l'achat de carburant et  la recharge lectrique, permettant un
   suivi prcis et une transparence totale des dpenses. Grce  cette
   solution, il est possible de rduire les cots et de bnficier d'un
   reporting dtaill pour mieux contrler les budgets allous aux
   dplacements professionnels. De plus, cette carte de recharge
   lectrique simplifie la gestion administrative en liminant la
   ncessit de traiter des justificatifs papier et en automatisant les
   processus de facturation. Elle est galement compatible avec un large
   rseau de stations- service et de bornes de recharge, ce qui assure une
   grande flexibilit et accessibilit pour les utilisateurs. En somme,
   adopter une carte carburant, c'est opter pour une solution moderne qui
   conjugue conomie, efficacit et praticit, tout en rpondant aux
   exigences d'une mobilit professionnelle durable et matrise.

(BUTTON) Pourquoi choisir la carte Fleet de TotalEnergies ?

   La carte carburant et recharge lectrique Fleet de TotalEnergies se
   distingue par sa capacit  rpondre aux besoins complexes des
   entreprises modernes. En
   combinant le paiement du carburant traditionnel, les achats en
   stations, les parkings et la recharge pour vhicules lectriques, cette
   carte de recharge lectrique offre une solution tout- en-un qui
   facilite la gestion de la mobilit professionnelle. Grce  une
   couverture gographique tendue, elle permet d'accder  un vaste
   rseau de stations-service, de bornes de recharge et de parkings,
   garantissant ainsi une grande flexibilit pour les dplacements. La
   carte Fleet de TotalEnergies propose galement des outils de suivi en
   temps rel et un reporting dtaill, facilitant ainsi le contrle des
   dpenses et l'optimisation des itinraires. De plus, elle offre des
   avantages tarifaires et des services d'assistance qui renforcent la
   scurit et la performance de vos dplacements. En choisissant cette
   carte carburant vous optez pour une solution moderne qui intgre  la
   fois le carburant et l'lectricit, simplifiant la gestion
   administrative tout en assurant une transparence complte sur les
   cots. C'est l'allie idale pour piloter efficacement la mobilit de
   votre entreprise.

(BUTTON) Comment la carte carburant et recharge lectrique Fleet peut-elle
aider  optimiser les cots de carburant et de recharge lectrique ?

   La carte carburant et recharge lectrique Fleet est spcialement conue
   pour optimiser la gestion des dpenses lies aux dplacements
   professionnels. En centralisant toutes les transactions, cette solution
   vous permet de suivre prcisment les consommations de carburant et les
   recharges lectriques grce  des outils de reporting performants. Vous
   pouvez ainsi identifier rapidement les opportunits d'conomies et
   ajuster les itinraires pour rduire les cots superflus. Le suivi des
   transactions et le paramtrage des dpenses vous aident galement 
   scuriser les paiements et  viter toute fraude ou dpassement de
   budget. Grce  une gestion centralise et transparente, la carte
   essence Fleet facilite la planification budgtaire et permet une
   analyse fine des dpenses. Ainsi, cette solution reprsente un
   vritable levier pour matriser les cots de mobilit tout en

(BUTTON) Comment utiliser la carte carburant et recharge lectrique Fleet ?

   L'utilisation de la carte carburant et recharge lectrique Fleet est
   pense pour tre simple et intuitive, permettant aux professionnels de
   se concentrer sur leur activit. Une fois active, la carte essence est
   immdiatement oprationnelle et peut tre utilise dans tous les points

(BUTTON) La carte GR existe-t-elle encore ?

   Historiquement, la carte GR tait une solution porte par TotalEnergies

   FAQs

Learn more about fleet telematics

   What is fleet telematics?

   Fleet telematics technology is a fleet management system designed to be
   the single command center for physical operations, enabling
   organizations to streamline every aspect of their fleet, field, and
   back-office operations.

   With fleet telematics, you can:
     * Unleash employee productivity with intuitive and personalized
       workflows for your drivers, admins, and technicians.
     * Minimize risk by delivering rich safety, compliance, maintenance,
       and security insights you can act on immediately to protect your
       fleet.
     * Maximize energy efficiency with our advanced fuel, energy, and
       emissions reporting and alerting.

   How does a fleet telematics solution help improve fleet management?

   With a fleet telematics solution like Samsara help improve the safety,
   compliance, and efficiency of your fleet. With GPS tracking for
   vehicles and assets, ELD compliance, and more, Samsara is a
   comprehensive telematics system that helps streamline fleet management.

   What are the benefits of a fleet telematics system?

   There are many benefits to a fleet telematics system, including:
     * Minimize fleet risk: Protect your people, assets, and your bottom
       line with actionable safety, compliance, maintenance, and security
       insights.
     * Increase worker productivity: Empower your teams with intuitive,
       personalized mobile app workflows for drivers, admins, and
       technicians.
     * Maximize energy efficiency: Future-proof your operations with
       advanced fuel analytics, energy management, and emissions planning.

   Is fleet telematics the same as GPS tracking?

   Fleet telematics software enables organizations to track the location
   of their fleet using real-time data, including to-the-second GPS data.
   With real-time GPS location tracking, fleets gain best-in-class
   visibility to improve route performance, fleet efficiency, and customer
   service.

   Some key features of real-time GPS tracking include:
     * Geofencing: Automatically get notified, via text or email when your
       assets arrive and leave specific locations, or when abnormal
       activity occurs.
     * Helicopter view: Eliminate guesswork with an instant aerial view of
       your assets. Easily aid route navigation and help authorities track
       stolen property.
     * Smart map overlays: Monitor weather and traffic or locate the
       nearest charging station. Quickly adapt to changing conditions so
       your drivers can stay on schedule.
     * Live location sharing: Deliver best-in-class customer service with
       live location links to equip your customers with accurate ETAs.

   How does fleet telematics help ensure ELD compliance?

   Amidst evolving regulations, fleet telematics systems are critical to
   helping fleet maintain compliance. Using fleet telematics, you gain
   real-time visibility into driver status, making it easy for drivers to
   log HOS and preventing ELD mandate violations. In addition, automated
   ELD workflows for drivers and robust reporting for admins across HOS,
   DVIR, and IFTA enable fleets to sail through audits with ease.

   How does fleet telematics software streamline preventative maintenance?

   By leveraging real-time vehicle diagnostics, fleets are able to improve
   preventative maintenance and reduce downtime. Proactively spot issues
   such as failing batteries and engine fault codes through over-the-air
   vehicle diagnostics. Avoid road breakdowns and quickly identify
   vehicles in need of service to protect vehicle health and extend
   vehicle lifetime.

   Fleet telematics systems like Samsara offer intuitive mobile workflows
   and to help improve vehicle maintenance. Samsara Vehicle Telematics
   help you prevent incidents before they happen with visibility into key
   driver and vehicle safety indicators like vehicle location, speed,
   harsh braking, and fault codes, while our Driver App helps you stay on
   top of potential defects with DVIRs.

   In addition, Samsara's offers a robust ecosystem of integrations,
   making it easy to connect your data to existing systems to ensure
   reporting accuracy, maximize administrative efficiency, and make
   smarter, informed decisions.

   How does fleet telematics technology improve fuel management?

   With fleet telematics, you gain real-time insights that enable you to
   reduce fuel consumption and improve energy efficiency. Samsara Vehicle
   Telematics helps you:
     * Motivate drivers to idle less and save more with personalized
       scoring and performance insights.
     * Cut down on fuel waste, energy use, and emissions with actionable
       alerts and advanced reporting.
     * As EV and emissions mandates become more prevalent, Samsara's
       automated EV assessments and customizable suitability criteria help
       you identify a path forward to vehicle electrification.

Corporate fleet solutions

Fleet management solutions for businesses

   Managing large fleets means dealing with complexity, costs,
   sustainability, data insights and more. Alphabet provides tailored
   solutions and innovative products and services, covering all aspects of
   business mobility, from funding to advanced mobility solutions.
   Talk to an expert
   Classification and benefits of fleet management

What is corporate fleet management?

   Corporate fleet management is about more than just vehicles. It's a
   balancing act covering cost control, driver satisfaction, emissions
   targets and operational reliability - all while keeping your business
   mobile. At Alphabet we help businessess navigate this complexity and
   make mobility easier, with the aim of building a future ready fleet
   strategy together.
   [di24-000236140.jpeg]
   Alphabet as a partner

Fleet management that fits your business needs

   At Alphabet, we combine expert consulting with digital innovation to
   help corporate fleet teams manage their vehicles efficiently. No matter
   how many you operate, we offer scalable services that are tailored to
   your specific needs, including CO[2] tracking, electrification and
   driver support.
   Our finance options

Funding options for every business

   Whatever your business goals are, Alphabet provides financial solutions
   that can be tailored to your operational needs.

Finance Lease

   A Finance Lease lets you keep vehicles on your balance sheet while
   enjoying fixed, flexible payments, with the option at the end to sell
   to a third party or continue using them under a peppercorn agreement (a
   percentage of the original invoice price).
   Learn more
   [di24-000233990.jpeg]

Salary Sacrifice

   Offer employees a cost-efficient car benefit that's easy to implement,
   with full support from our expert team and access to a wide range of
   low and zero-emission vehicles.
   Learn more
   [di24-000236250.jpeg]

Sale and Leaseback

   This can give your business the immediate financial advantage of
   releasing the value tied up in your owned vehicles.
   Learn more
   [di23-000162263.jpeg]

Contract Purchase

   Under this fixed-term, fixed amount agreement, you have all the
   advantages of a managed vehicle, plus the option to buy it at the end
   of the contract.
   Learn more
   [di24-000233952.jpeg]

Got questions? We've got answers

(BUTTON) How does corporate fleet management work?

   Enterprise fleet management is about more than just vehicles. It's a
   balancing act covering cost control, driver satisfaction, emissions
   targets and operational reliability all while keeping your business
   mobile. At Alphabet we help corporations navigate this complexity and
   make mobility easier, with the aim of together building a future ready
   fleet strategy.

(BUTTON) What is corporate fleet management?

   Enterprise fleet management is a balancing act covering cost control,
   driver satisfaction, emissions targets and operational reliability all
   while keeping your business mobile.

(BUTTON) What is a corporate fleet?

   A corporate fleet is a collection of vehicles such as cars, vans, or
   specialised equipment, that a business or organisation owns or leases
   to support its operations and transport goods, services, or people.

(BUTTON) How many cars can you have in an enterprise fleet?

   The size of an enterprise fleet can vary greatly depending on the type
   of business, but generally they can consist of anything from 1 to
   10,000 cars.

(BUTTON) What is corporate fleet management software?

   Enterprise fleet management software is a suite of digital tools
   designed to assist in the management of the vehicles. They provide a
   range of functions to improve decision making and operations including
   tracking, maintenance, driver monitoring, fuel management and
   reporting.
   (BUTTON) Show more Show less

   oversight and cost controls.

Fleet Management Services

   accident management, driver safety, fuel and EV charging, tolling,
   vehicle telematics, driver tax compliance and more.

Sustainable Fleet Solutions for a Greener Future

As businesses increasingly prioritize sustainability, Wheels is committed to
helping you transition to an environmentally friendly fleet.  We offer
comprehensive fleet management solutions for electric vehicles (EVs),
including:

     * Vehicle Selection and Acquisition: Our experts can guide you
       through the process of selecting and acquiring the right EVs for
       your needs.
     * Charging Infrastructure: We can help you plan and implement the
       necessary charging infrastructure to support your EV fleet.
     * Driver Training: We provide comprehensive driver training programs
       to ensure your team is prepared to operate and maintain EVs.
     * Software Integration: Our fleet management software seamlessly
       integrates with EVs, providing real-time monitoring, charging
       status updates, and performance data.
